# Global Strategy

# Quantamental Signal Ideas - Top & Bottom Ranked Stocks

## Details on the methodology/model can be found in our Q-Series report

## What are the model’s recommendations today?

Based on the stock-level scores and an optimisation for portfolio balance, we find that the highest-scoring stocks tend to come from sectors including Financials, Communication Services, Cap Goods, Energy and Utilities. The lowest-scoring stock list is dominated by Consumer Services, Staples, Food Beverage & Tobacco, Household Personal Products and Semis stocks. Do note these recommendations can differ from the fundamental views of our equity strategy team.

## Digging into the details, what key message is each signal cohort suggesting?

• Price momentum continues to slow across regions, especially in Europe & China. Overall, the most favoured sectors are Financials, Utilities & Energy, when the least favoured are Real Estate, Health Care & Consumer oriented sectors.

• Broad positioning keeps getting more and more supportive for Chinese stocks, from a regional perspective, and for Real Estate from a sector/industry perspective. Outside of Australia & China, our model is sending warnings on "crowded" overweights in Tech.

• Earnings expectations and momentum have consistently improved vs. last month. Utilities, and to a lesser extent Staples, experienced a significant improvement in their EPS outlook. Defensive sectors (excl. Utilities) are still struggling to move up in the ranking, despite some early signs of economic deceleration in the macro data.

• The OECD G20 Composite Leading index is still pointing to 'expansion' as the most likely regime in H2'2024, although the probability of a 'slowdown' has picked up in Q2 (+5% points). Cyclical sectors continue to be favoured in our regime model.

• On valuation, Financials, Utilities & Staples remain the cheapest sectors in our adjusted 12mth fwd PE model, when Health Care, Tech & Materials remain the most expensive. Relative valuations have been fairly stable in 2024.

• Global PMI new orders had a little set-back in June, declining from 52.7 in May to 52.0.
The most (negatively) affected industries were Energy, Utilities and Industrials.

## What are the industries whose rankings have changed the most?

Overall, the model is still displaying a risk-on stance, favouring cyclicals over defensives with a non-negligeable beta exposure. Changes vs. last month are minor. Staples and Health care exhibit slight improvements in their scoring, from relatively low levels, when Industrials, Materials and Consumer Discretionary face small deteriorations, from relatively high levels.

UBS publishes a report that utilizes a quantamental framework for single stocks known as the "Quantamental Signals Strategy." The model uses 11 high-performing indicators to filter a trading signal for each stock globally. An easy-to-read dashboard that includes all stocks listed on the S&P 500, Eurostoxx 600, MSCI China, Nikkei 225, S&P/ASX 200 and S&P/TSX is available, and you should contact your UBS sales representative or the Quantitative Research team at qa@ubs.com for details and pricing.

## What our US (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/ lowest ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Joseph Spak on General Motors: We are positive on GM believing they remain disciplined in running the business and managing volume to protect pricing power, are mostly insulated from international pressures, see upside to estimates and continue to execute on their capital allocation policy.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Dennis Geiger on Starbucks Corp: We believe US traffic challenges could persist over the near to medium term as strategic plans take time to gain traction and as consumers may need time to digest elevated pricing. Even with an undemanding valuation and still solid brand positioning, limited visibility into the timing of an inflection in US and China sales could weigh on shares.

Jay Sole on Nike Inc: We believe Nike is undergoing a major business reset which will take years to play out. This reset will limit upward EPS revision potential and probably weigh on Nike's P/E. We still believe Nike has the brand strength and balance sheet to ultimately return to +M-HSD% sales growth rates and a mid-teens EBIT margin over the long term.

Jay Sole on VF Corp: We believe VFC's stock price will stop falling once the 2nd derivative of its sales and EPS growth trends transitions to getting less bad from getting worse. However, we believe an inflection point looks far away and still see significant risk VF's turnaround fails to gain traction. This justifies the stock's current valuation, in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Manav Gupta on Bunge Global: US crush spread - which was at \$35/ton at the start of June - has now improved to \$75/ ton. We see this as a major positive for BG. With a rebound on crush margins, we see BG's \$9/shr annual guidance as conservative.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Faham Baig on Altria Group: We have a Sell rating on Altria. The group is facing headwinds from a more challenged US market (100% of EBIT), where it is investing behind lower-priced Marlboro Black, and should result in Smokeable sales and OCI declining in FY24. The company is also attempting to catch up in next-generation products (NGP), which will prove difficult, in our view, resulting in Altria's share of the nicotine category declining. Whilst the recently announced buyback has resulted in the shares bouncing, we do not believe it is sustainable.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Energy

Josh Silverstein on Coterra Energy: One of our Top Picks, we like the combination of oil volume upside, nat gas leverage, strong balance sheet, high shareholder return profile, and low valuation.

Manav Gupta on Kinder Morgan Inc: We expect demand for Nat gas would grow to support power needs of AI driven data centers.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Timothy Chiodo on Paypal Holdings Inc: We are Neutral on the stock given gross profit growth challenges associated with both the core Branded Checkout button and the impacts associated with the migration of three legacy business (PayPal Pro, PayPal Here, and PayPal Invoices). While we expect continued gross profit growth pressures (albeit partially due to factors that will either partially lap or become less meaningful into 2024), uncertainty around operating expenses required (initiatives underway, expectation for a degree of ramping in 2H 2024), and increasing competitive pressures (existing concerns around Shop Pay, Apple Pay, etc., which are recently heightened by Apple Pay’s move to desktop outside iOS) to limit the attractiveness of the shares near-term. The path for PayPal shares to become more attractive would require additional confidence in a more meaningful (and durable) resumption in total company gross profit growth. For balance, we note near-term results (Q2 2024) are expected to be strong, given management’s recent comments around quarter-to-date trends.

Brennan Hawken on Franklin Resources Inc: While BEN has acquired some solid alts franchises recently (ie Lexington, BSP), their alts growth has not been sufficient to offset the outflows from their traditional, active business, resulting in negative organic growth for the past five years. BEN’s bond capabilities are robust, but performance across WAMCO has been disappointing, which suggests that they will not benefit as much from a recovery in demand for fixed income in a higher for longer environment.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

AJ Rice on HCA Health care Inc: The leading provider of healthcare services with a portfolio of hospitals concentrated in the fastest growing markets across the country. HCA consistently posts volume gains that exceed those of the industry at large and is likely to see upside to earnings over the course of 2024. HCA is benefitting from an improving market for nursing and physician services costs.

AJ Rice on Cigna Group: Cigna consistently delivers EPS growth of 10-14% annually through a combination of high single digit organic growth and capital deployment. The company’s core businesses of employer sponsored health insurance and pharmacy benefit services offer highly predictable and steady growth. Cigna generates strong cash flow from its fee-based business which it is using to repurchase nearly \$5 bln in stock this year.

Dan Leonard on Mettler Toledo International: -We believe challenges in some end markets where MTD has unique exposure (e.g., China) will limit its near-term growth. Outlook remains cautious on weak underlying end market demand and we assume a return to growth in 2H’24 primarily on easier comps.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Ashwani Verma on Biogen Inc: We see little potential for stock outperformance in the next 6-12 months. Cost-cutting is well-understood, and upcoming readouts are high risk with limited potential for value inflection. We expect Leqembi to lead in MCI/Mild Alzheimer’s and become a multi-billion dollar product, but we don’t expect a major sales inflection in 2024. For the stock to work, Leqembi needs to show steady growth and have a clear market path for SubQ use.

Dan Leonard on Illumina Inc: -Illumina remains a company in transition with a balance of structural challenges and opportunities. While we see the potential for an earnings power story with a commitment to restore margins, execution on these commitments remains early days.

Dan Leonard on West Pharmaceutical Services: -While we see growth opportunities for WST (e.g., increased adoption of high-value products, Annex 1 revisions, and GLP-1 tailwinds), we believe destocking headwinds, margin pressures, and capacity constraints at WST’s facilities could limit near-term upside.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Steven Fisher on Cummins Inc: We rate CMI a Buy. NA truck demand has been more resilient than expected over the past 6-12 months, given how weak freight rates have been, and we think there is strong visibility to the next 2-3 year cycle and a 2026 peak. This visibility is a function of historical cyclical patterns, and the effect of a pre-buy ahead of new 2027 emissions regulations by the EPA that will significantly increase the cost of a truck. CMI also has several levers for generating outsized margin expansion over the coming upcycle, including improving profitability in Accelera, reduced R&D in the engine segment as new models are launched in 2025/26, and improved pricing from the introduction of the new models.

Gavin Parsons on Lockheed Martin: Lockheed Martin is the largest US Defense Prime contractor, and benefits from increases in defense spend related to elevated geopolitical tension. Its largest program, the F-35, has experienced delays and cash flow looks stable over the next few years, which drives our Neutral view on the stock.

Alex Kramm on Verisk Analytics: VRSK as a key solutions provider in the defensive P&C insurance market with an attractive growth profile. We believe the increased focus on the core insurance business could unlock new upside opportunities, which could ultimately drive the company to a higher sustainable growth profile. Valuation remains our only hesitation, so we would look for any dislocation to become more bullish on the stock.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Damian Karas on Johnson Controls International: We recently downgraded the stock to Neutral, as we see a difficult path to FY24 guide and growth continuing to lag peers. Potential divestitures of non-core assets (25% of portfolio) represent modest upside to our PT (Link)

Kevin McVeigh on Paychex Inc: We are Neutral the stock because 1) durable growth + FCF generation—constructive longer-term, near-term we believe the stock isn’t discounting enough macro concern, 2) stock has been option for yield investors given durable FCF, leading margins, amid 3.35% dividend yield, 3) levered to SMBs, which could be challenging in late 2024-25 as Paychex anniversaries down-market hiring which absorbed upmarket slack, and 4) \$125 PT—based on 24x FY26E EPS—compared to 3-year range of 23.2x-35.2x + average of 28.7x. Target multiple seems ok on cloud transition–PAYX \~7.5x TARGET EV/sales. Risks: SMB employment, SaaS-based HCM competition. Price target based on 2-year multiple on our FY26 estimates.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

David Vogt on Hewlett Packard: We are Neutral on HPE as its business is sluggish in 2 important end markets.

David Vogt on Arista Networks Inc: Arista has operating momentum but we are Neutral rated as investor/expectations are way above Street #s and the valuation (>40x P/E) is at an all time high despite \$0 from AI this year.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

Kevin McVeigh on Cognizant Tech Solutions: We are Neutral on the stock. With favorable positioning following significant investments in digital IT Services, Cognizant should at least shadow its share of industry-wide IT Services spending, which is expected to grow in the high single digits over the next five years. \$74 PT is based on \~14x 2026E EPS—this is more than modest discount to its ten-year average of 17.0x/11.0x. 2 year multiple based on FY26 estimates, rolled forward by Cost of Equity to derive 1 year Price Target.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Daniel Major on Newmont Corp: After material underperformance vs the gold price over the last 2-3yrs we believe NEM is at an inflection point. We believe 2024 guidance is achievable (vs missing guidance last 3yrs) and this will provide strong operational momentum in 2H24; this combined with strong gold price and asset sales is expected to accelerate deleveraging and cash returns.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Avalonbay Communities: We have a Neutral rating on AvalonBay. The company’s Coastal portfolio should continue to deliver same store growth in the top end of the peer group given favorable supply/demand dynamics. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for AVB in 2024. Additionally, we expect strong value creation from the development pipeline.

Michael Goldsmith on Simon Property Group: We have a Neutral rating on Simon Property. The company’s unique portfolio of premium mall and outlet assets allow it to engage with retailers across many dimensions; full-price to outlet, domestic and international. As a result, SPG has successfully improved occupancy and rents. However, recent share price outperformance and volatility around SPG’s retail investment income leaves us on the sidelines.

Michael Goldsmith on Federal Realty Invs Trust: We have a Neutral rating on Federal Realty. FRT’s portfolio is concentrated in markets with robust demographic profiles. Given pressure on the lower end consumer, we expect these locations to see relatively strong customer demand which should support elevated tenant demand, occupancy, and lease spreads. Additionally, FRT’s balance sheet enables it to be one of the only potential buyers for large Strip Center assets.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Alex Kramm on CBRE Group: We view CBRE as the blue chip in the CRE brokerage space. The company has diversified well into more recurring outsourcing businesses, which should have stable and attractive growth profiles. From a cyclical perspective, the stock should benefit from a thawing of capital markets activity and a more active leasing market.

Michael Goldsmith on Mid America Apartment: We have a Sell rating on MAA. Demand has held up well for Sunbelt Apartment properties, but supply has limited pricing power. As supply ramps up in 2H’24, we expect the supply/demand balance to favor renters. Supply should start to ease in 2025, but we don’t expect significant improvement in Sunbelt same-store growth metrics until 2026.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

William Appicelli on Eversource Energy: Utility repositioning underway to de-lever and refocus on electric and gas delivery. Closing offshore wind asset sales this year and potential sale of water utility raises \$2.6 bln combined and stabilizes ratings based on 14% FFO/debt. November recovery of Automated Meter investment in Connecticut a catalyst to close 16% P/E discount on our \$4.87 in ’25.

William Appicelli on Public Service Enterprise: Utility business mix that includes ownership in 3 nuclear plants and potential to improve pricing through AI/data center contract that is consistent with state energy goals. Electric and gas rate case settlement discussions scheduled this Summer which could result in a catalyst.

William Appicelli on CMS Energy: Predominantly utility business mix that has one of best regulatory environments in Michigan that supports consistent, above average EPS growth of 7%. Michigan legislation in 2023 set framework to earn equity return on renewable purchased power agreements.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

William Appicelli on Southern Co: The stock is a large cap, high quality utility with a strong balance sheet, supportive regulation and a strong tail wind from an increase in the demand for power, particularly at its largest subsidiary, Georgia Power. However, the stock trades at one of the largest P/E premiums on 2026 earnings at nearly 18%, despite only modestly above average earnings growth over the next five years.

Gregg Orrill on American Water Works: Diversified U.S. water utility in 13 states with 7.5% EPS growth and 2% yield. 10% P/E premium on our \$5.63 in ’25 represents limited upside.

## What our European (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Ondrej Cabejsek on Telia Co: Telia offers good momentum with material deleveraging (2.7x ND/EBITDA at 2Q23 to c2.0x at 4Q24E) thanks to the sale of Denmark, NWC normalisation and EBITDAaL acceleration, consensus upgrades (UBSe on average 11% ahead on 2024-26 OFCF), and attractive value (7.0% dividend yield). Consensus underestimates the restructuring and content momentum in Telia’s TV unit as well as the impact on FCF from lower leverage and interest rates. Telia also carries optionality on the TV unit, where strategic options are being assessed into 2025. In September, Telia's new management will present a strategy update, hence we do not expect 2Q24 to be a major catalyst. At the CMD, we expect focus on: 1) continued cost cutting including the 2026 shut down of copper, which is currently yielding material savings at Telefonica and Telenor; 2) NWC volatility optimisation; 3) TV & Media restructuring; 4) mid-term capex outlook; 5) mid-term OFCF growth path; 6) capital allocation following the deleveraging to very low end of policy (2.0-2.5x ND/EBITDA excl. hybrids which are worth 0.3x). On the last point, we expect management to communicate DPS growth from 2025 with potential fibre bolt-on deals on the back of expected regional FTTH regulation in Sweden.

Polo Tang on Deutsche Telekom: Deutsche Telekom (Buy, PT €27.6) is hitting new multiyear highs amid a healthy backdrop for US mobile and TMUS hitting new highs. Importantly, technical overhangs at DT have been removed post the recent government placing and we remain confident on fundamentals and think the stock can continue to re-rate. Despite the recent re-rating, the shares are still relatively cheap and haven’t fully reflected the TMUS rally. DT group trades on 13x PE and offers >10% pa EPS growth with a 9.5% EFCF yield. The historic EFCF yield has been c7% but we think the DT shares are yet to catch-up with the doubling in FCF over the past three years post the TMUS/ Sprint merger.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Ondrej Cabejsek on Infrastructure Wireless Italiane: In our recent deep dive (link) we explore Inwit’s exposure the slowing Italian market. We see mid-term positive outcomes for Inwit in case of consolidation that could deleverage and/or increase returns at its core anchors customers, and thus improve their ability to invest. Currently, however, Italy is subject to uncertainty around potential in-market consolidation of telecommunications operators, and pressure on returns at key FWA customers. We believe this will continue to be the case in the near-term, and therefore that sentiment won’t immediately improve around Inwit’s future growth potential. The company offers a solid, c5% dividend yield at a declining leverage (4.7x pro-forma ND/EBITDA) and upside in the case of a minority buyout, but we think the latter may be less likely currently with the aforementioned uncertainty around growth in the market. We also believe that beyond the 2026 guidance period, during which Inwit can simultaneously deleverage and increase shareholder remuneration, FCFE growth is capped by expiring tax benefits that are likely to eat up c60% of EBITDAaL growth between 2026 and 2030, we estimate. We therefore believe that Inwit may eventually have to choose between re-leveraging (albeit well below its leverage policy range) or keeping dividends/buybacks.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Jarrod Castle on Accor SA: Accor is due to report results on 25th April which we think will be reassuring on 2Q trading and summer outlook. We continue to see the scope for capital returns and re-rating of multiple.

Zuzanna Pusz on Hermes: RMS continues to be seen as the luxury sector's most resilient, unique supply-driven equity story, with demand for its leather goods division (\~41% of sales in 2023) exceeding supply. As such we expect 2024 to be a year when the company could consolidate further its position as one of the sector's most defensive stocks amid an industry-wide slow-down with our estimated +14% OSG vs. +4% for peers given more pricing and volume resilience, and very limited EBIT margin pressure (-50bp y/y driven primarily by non-recurrence of hedging gains). RMS remains our top-pick.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Zuzanna Pusz on Hugo Boss: We think Hugo Boss’s sales growth outperformance vs. peers can continue; however, the company won't be immune to the current macro headwinds and forecast +6% FX-neutral sales growth in 2024E, driven by commercial opportunities opened up by the casualisation of the offering, supported by strong brand investments. The associated higher promotions in the market will likely mask the gross margin improvement due to lower freight and product costs, which together with ongoing investments in the business will limited operating leverage in the ST. Despite shares trading at a PEG ratio of \~1.0x we believe the market may be hesitant to support a higher valuation in the uncertain context. Neutral.

Zuzanna Pusz on Burberry Group Plc: We have a Sell rating on the stock. We are cautious on BRBY as we continue to see downside risks to earnings amid the brand’s turnaround, the execution of which further heightened by the uncertain sector backdrop with no signs of brand momentum inflection. Key points in our investment thesis: (1) so far muted reaction of the trade and consumers to the new brand aesthetics and price positioning; (2) concerns around the success of turnaround without the company abandoning its ambitious profitability targets (\~25% EBIT margin) in the ST, to sustainably accelerate its LT topline growth; (3) ultimate decision to be made regarding the brand’s future ie to either meaningfully step up its investments to accelerate LT topline growth or abandon the brand elevation strategy for good, which would also have consequences for its LT margin potential as well as valuation; (4) high relative to peers exposure to the outlet channel on both sales (UBSe \~40%) and EBIT (UBSe \~60%); (5) according to UBS Evidence Lab's Global Luxury Goods Consumer survey, Burberry so far does not see sustainable evidence of brand momentum inflection.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Staples

Sreedhar Mahamkali on Associated British Foods and M&S Group: We are Neutral on both, and with all the UK reporting we are seeing weather related challenges with the summer weather (or lack of it). M&S might still benefit from earnings recovery though.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Joshua Stone on Galp Energia: We rate the stock Neutral. The discovery of >10 billion boe of oil in place in Namibia has materially improved the equity story and offers a new avenue for production growth in the late 2020s, diluting potential challenges associated with the security situation in Mozambique and eventual decline in Brazil. Key catalysts include a potential upgrade to reservoir estimates in Namibia after appraisal and a part disposal to another industry player to help derisk the prospect further. The current valuation of the shares holds us back on being more positive on the stock.

Henri Patricot on Repsol SA: We rate Repsol Buy, as we see the company as likely to surprise positively on cashflow generation which should allow to maintain high shareholder returns and transition towards low-carbon faster than peers. We expect the company to continue raising the dividends and maintain buybacks at >€1bn p.a., providing a sector-leading distribution yield of \~14% over 2024-25.

Joshua Stone on ENI Spa: We rate the stock Buy. Eni has traded at a 20% EV/DACF average discount to peers over the past five years, reflecting a weaker performance on returns driven by cost overruns and a bias towards a weak Italian downstream market. The stock now trades in line with peers, but we think a premium is now warranted. A new development approach has reduced the risk of project delays in the upstream, natural gas trading margins are supported by restructuring, the transition businesses are gaining scale, and the refining business is being replaced by a higher-returning biofuels offering.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Joshua Stone on OMV AG: OMV shares are now yielding over 11% in dividends this year. The stock looks cheap. However, in our view, the challenge is a lack of momentum with free cash flow likely to get worse before it gets better, and continued uncertainty regarding future M&A. We have Neutra rating.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Michal Potyra on Powszechny Zaklad Ubezpiecze: PZU is currently rated Neutral and the recent operating trends and comments were not very supportive. Valuation remains quite low but we don’t see an obvious positive catalysts.

Adele Palama on Poste Italiane: We rate PT with a Buy. We are constructive on the ability of Poste to report a healthy 24-28E earning progression though we still retain a certain degree of conservativeness vs 24-28E management revenues targets. We identify volume growth in the parcel business and activity volume in payment as the two areas of major upside. The stock is trading at c8x EPS25E from consensus, at discount versus historical levels and selected peers while offering an attractive 24-26E dividend yield of c8-9% with 65% payout. A sustained capital growth expected (c7% p.a.) will pave the way for a potential higher dividend, with Poste having the flexibility to raise its payout up to 70-80%, we believe, leading to almost 10% capital return yield.

Michal Potyra on Bank Pekao: We recently downgraded the stock to Neutral after a strong run.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Michael Werner on London Stock Exchange: We rate LSEG Neutral. While we view the long-term opportunities for LSEG attractively, particularly on a 3-5 year time horizon, we expect a lack of positive catalysts for the remainder of 2024 will result in the shares remaining range-bound. Given LSEG's strong multiple expansion in 2023 (its forward P/ E multiple increased from 20x to 25x), we think we need meaningful upgrades to cons EPS before LSEG's shares can benefit from further multiple expansion.

Johan Ekblom on Jyske Bank: Jyske has performed well YTD and shares now lie in line with NDA and Danske at \~8x 2025E EPS. However, with limited levels of excess capital, capital distribution potential lags peers. Adjusting for excess capital Jyske remains at a premium valuation to peers leaving limited upside in our view.

Michael Werner on Abrdn Plc: We rate abrdn a Sell. Fund flows have been persistently negative for abrdn’s asset management business in recent years and we expect weak fund performance to result in further outflows in the coming years (through 2027). We also expect declining interest rates to act as a headwind to its platform and adviser businesses, where NII contributes c50% of revenues.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Xian Deng on Ipsen: Midcap pharma company that grows at above sector rate but trades at a discount. Ipsen is one of the least binary stock in our SMID pharma/biotech coverage as the growth is largely driven by approved assets. We like Dysport (Ipsen’s botox equivalent for cosmetics use), and we believe its consumer characteristics could drive sustained long term growth, and the longevity of growth is often underappreciated. Elafibranor launch is a key investor focus in H2'24 and a successful launch could install investor confidence in management's execution ability, a "must have" for the story to work, in our view.

Olivier Calvet on Gerresheimer AG: Gerresheimer is a drug containment provider active 1) in all the GLP-1 races, with an attractive leading position in Novo Nordisk's next high profile drug CagriSema, that offers optionality, 2) with an inflection point ahead: historically it has not shown much organic sales growth (except the last 3Y but in its lower margin segment Primary Packaging Glass), margin expansion (flat over the last 3Y) or much cash generation (cash conversion of c. 0% at FCF level over the last 3Y, amid GLP-1 related capex). We expect the capex to sales to decrease and sales growth in Gerresheimer’s higher margin plastics and devices segment to drive margin and FCF uplift. We highlight the cagrisema readout in 4Q23 in particular as a key catalyst.

Xian Deng on UCB SA: UCB represents a highly attractive profile that deserves premium valuation. We forecast sector leading growth (ex-Novo) for UCB with no patent cliff for this decade, and the growth is largely driven by de-risked, approved assets Bimzelx, Rystiggo and Zilbrysq. While valuation has caught up in the recent months driven by strong Bimzelx launch, continued launch progression of new drugs and pipeline readouts (10 readouts due 2024) could lead to further upside. We highlight UCB's antia-synuclein drug UCB0599 with P2 Parkinson's data due Q2'24. While high risk, we see this as a potential needle mover as its oral formulation is a key differentiating factor versus competitor asset, in our view.

Sebastian Vogel on Galenica: We expect that the company will see above long-term average sales growth in the short-term on the back of previous acquisitions. This should allow for scale driven margin benefits. Moreover, latest flu developments in Australia paint a supportive picture for Galenica‘s OTC demand prospects once the flu season will start in late autumn. This should be also margin supportive on the back of positive mix effects. In addition, we see previous regulatory devopments also positive for the company‘s future demand prospects. At the same time, Galenica‘s dividend yield remains above its long-term average.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Rory McKenzie on SPIE SA: A defensive compounder with exposure to sustainability driven themes, trading at an attractive valuation. We see a c5% organic revenue CAGR achievable over the mid-term given rising demand for SPIE’s services to e.g. improve building efficiency, drive the energy transition, and improve connectivity. With scope for continued bolt-on M&A in fragmented markets, we therefore see a potential 8-9% adj net income CAGR that looks undervalued today on c11x EV/EBITA.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Rory McKenzie on Rentokil Initial Plc: We continue to see earnings risk to the downside, with UBS estimates -1-8% below consensus in FY'24-26. FY'24 organic growth forecasts look largely reset and the 'growth gap' to Rollins has likely troughed, but we think consensus at >4% pa NA Pest organic from FY'25e+ looks stretched given integration disruption, intense competition, and the shift towards a fairly unproven large branch model. We expect higher 'reinvestment' requirements to drive growth, and so remain well below even pushed-out margin targets. However, a c25% pullback since a Q3'23 growth slowdown has ignited a new debate around value and strategic change.

Rory McKenzie on Ashtead Group Plc: While there is still a significant long-term growth opportunity to expand and consolidate the US Equipment Rental market, in the nearterm growth prospects are limited by slowing end-markets (e.g. US non-residential construction) and a catch-up in industry supply. Normalising used equipment prices also present additional earnings headwinds, and overall we see a period of below-trend earnings growth ahead.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on Infineon Technologies: We believe Infineon is likely to experience an upcycle in 2025 in industrial and automotive and well positioned to benefit from electrification going forward.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Michael Briest on Sage Group: Lagged impact of weaker SMB spending power and fading pricing power as inflation rates fall are risks to topline growth. Longer-term, the group’s incomplete cloud-native product footprint creates a competitive disadvantage.

Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on Ericsson: We believe telco capex spending will continue to be challenged and therefore impact Ericsson revenues in 2024 and into 2025. We are also cautious on the structural risk posted by OpenRAN.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Patrick Rafaisz on EMS Chemie Holding: We are Neutral on the stock. EMS has reinvented itself as a beneficiary of the electrification trend in autos. A 10% higher content per vehicle, above-average exposure to China (17%) and dedicated growth investments should hence allow the company to outgrow global build rates in the future with EBIT margins recovering to c28%. With a best-in-class ROIC post tax of 37% and a strong balance sheet (no bank debt to speak of, >CHF300m net liquidity), the company is in solid shape. However, this is counterbalanced by increased uncertainty near-term from softening build rates in Europe and the risk of increasing price pressure from Western OEMs. Hence, with the shares already trading at a (deserved) 35% premium over selected European quality chemicals names on EV/EBITDA risk/reward looks balanced. See link.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Samuel Perry on Lanxess AG: Lanxess is highly levered (>4x ND/EBITDA in FY24) and FCF generation is low so the ability to reduce is limited in the absence of a cyclical recovery (which we do not see signs of currently). There are also risks pertaining to the FY EBITDA guidance, which we are 4% below.

Priyanka Patelon K+S AG: We are Sell rated on K+S given it is a high cost producer of potash (70% of current production in Germany) and short term we see limited recovery in the potash price, and therefore earnings & FCF, with unsupportive farmer economics; UBSe 4%/10% below 2024/25 cons EBITDA. K+S also faces longer-term earnings risk due to increasing potash supply from BHP (2026).

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Zachary Gauge on British Land Co Plc: Despite a strong performance since February-2024 we see further upside in the name as UK values are showing clear evidence of stabilization. The company guide towards what is in our view a relatively conservative 3- 5% ERV growth for FY25, which with stable yields will start to drive a revaluation in the portfolio. In addition to the upside from the capitalized rental growth, we are seeing evidence of inward yield shift within the retail warehouse market with prime yields in by 50 bps since Feb-24.

Charles Boissier on Merlin Properties: We remain buyers, attracted by the data center market's structural tailwinds, driving development profit of €1.0bn (13% of NAV) by 2029 on our numbers hence data centers acceleration deserves capital. The rest of the company’s portfolio is also performing well, fuelled by solid demand in the shopping center and logistics segments. Merlin is one of the few companies where credit rating was upgraded, with balance sheet screening solid on our analysis.

Tommaso Operto on PSP Swiss Property: We continue to like PSP due to its: 1) solid balance sheet (FY23 net LTV of 34.7%), 2) hard to replicate prime portfolio, and 3) predictable capital allocation strategy from a well-regarded management team.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Charles Boissier on Castellum AB: The prospects of further Riksbank cuts has fuelled a rally in Swedish real estate stocks . Yet this is concomitant with slowing growth, evident from Castellum’s results . Additionally, in light of the financial situation of Castellum, we forecast dividend will remain suspended.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on Enel: We liked the plan presented in the November CMD, with focus on cash generation, cost reduction, capex cuts and potential dividend upside. We see the company delivering higher than expected earnings growth through 26 and with >8bn lower net capex than in the previous plan. Stock continues trading at an unjustified discount in light of superior dividend, attractive growth and balance sheet derisking. Q1 results surprised positive and give confidence on guidance delivery this year.

Wanda Serwinowska on Engie: In our view, the reasons for Engie's historically low multiples are being addressed systematically, with: (i) volatile commodity-sensitive activities disposed; (ii) opaque energy services activities disposed; (iii) a nuclear exit pathway agreed in Belgium, with liabilities capped at last; (iv) a company-wide cost & simplification programme under way; (v) new multi-year earnings and dividend guidance issued; and (vi) a management focus on consistent delivery. The result should be a more stable and predictable business that no longer deserves the current discount to utility sector multiples, but track records take time to be established and this is likely to be a multi-year process. Political risk in France seems to be the main concern among investors at the moment. On our estimates, France accounts for >50% of Engie's EV, however the vast majority is represented by regulated gas networks.

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on Iberdrola: The company is executing its 23-26E strategic plan, which includes a solid €41bn capex plan (gross) that further reinforces its regulated profile: almost ⅔ of capex will be in grids and around ⅓ in renewables. This should allow for mid-to-high-single-digit growth at EBITDA and net profit over 2022-26. We deem a premium valuation well deserved given superior EPS growth, attractive dividend, weight of regulated/contracted activities and well-diversified portfolio, combined with consistent delivery.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Mark Freshney on Severn Trent Plc: Severn Trent (PT 2,340p, Sell) trades on a c19% premium to March 2025 RAB, versus Pennon on c1% discount and UU on a c7% premium. We prefer Buy-rated PNN (PT 870p, Buy). SVT has carried a premium valuation over UU - the major peer - for much of the past decade. The premium was due to more asset base growth, higher ODI performance and smaller EV than UU. The totex for 2025- 2030 period in PR24 draft determination has been cut back by 15% from business plan (vs. PNN on 10% cut and UU on 16%), with an allowed return of 3.66% CHIP real plus a 30bps uplift for SVT and PNN. The FYmar25E dividend yield on the current policy is 4.7%; in-line with that of the sector. The upside risk for SVT is that the company gets a higher totex/return in potential CMA appeal for PR24 determination, or adapts and finds a way to continue to earn outcome delivery incentives.

## What our Chinese (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Communication Services

Felix Liu on Bilibili (9626 HK): We like the stock into the upcoming Q224 results in Aug due to the strong recent new game launch performance.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Samuel Wang on Pop Mart International (9992 HK): Gaining popularity in southeast asia market and product category expansion.

Paul Gong on Geely Automobile Holdings (175 HK): We have a Buy rating because of continuous market share gain and earnings growth.

Nora Min on Fuyao Glass Industry (3606 HK): We are upbeat on Fuyao’s earnings growth resilience amid the fast changing auto industry, as: 1) binding with all mainstream OEMs (global market share above 35%), Fuyao’s customer structure is well balanced, the fluctuation of any single OEM’s sales would not impact its earnings notable; 2) with rising contribution of high-value added product, such as all-glass roof, coating glass, and encapsulated glass, Fuyao’s ASP is likely to rise steadily even in the industry competition. In addition, with a well-developed plant in the US, we believe Fuyao is less vulnerable to the potential tariff hike.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Paul Gong on Dongfeng Motor Group (489 HK) and Guangzhou Automobile Group (2238 HK): Deteriorating volume and profitability continues for both.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Staples

Christine Peng on Uni President China (220 HK): We hold a positive view towards UPC in view of accelerating revenue growth led by the beverage division, a more rational noodle market competition and strong dividend payouts.

Henry Liu on JD Health International (6618 HK): We expect top line growth to recover notably YoY and QoQ from Q2, driven by growth in online prescription drug sales and COVID-driven high-base effect phasing out.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Staples

Christine Peng on Nongfu Spring (9633 HK): We view Nongfu’s much-higher-than peers’ profitability and return as unsustainable in view of its market share losses in the bottled water segment and increasingly intensifying competitions in the beverage segment.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Energy

Amily Guo on Petrochina Co Ltd (857 HK): The global oil & gas team recently raised the 2024 oil price forecast to \$84/bbl from \$83/bbl on tigher H224 oil market. The global oil & gas team has pushed back the return of OPEC+ bbls to 2Q25 from 3Q24 and see a large deficit of 1.4Mb/d in 3Q24 as oil demand bounces back seasonally while supply growth remains limited.We remain bullish on China’s Big-3 oil majors (CNOOC, Sinopec and PetroChina) because: 1) UBS remains optimistic on oil prices in H224; 2) China oil majors’ H-share valuations remain attractive compared to overseas peers, especially US oil companies; 3) High dividends. Sinopec/PetroChina/CNOOC’s 2023 dividend payouts were 75% (including buyback)/ 50%/44%; and 4) historically, Chinese oil companies have outperformed the market in a tough macro context. We prefer the H shares over the A shares.

Amily Guo on China Oilfield Services (2883 HK): For oil services, in the short run, we believe the Saudi's rig deal suspension will put some pressure on rig utilisation and day rates in the ME in the next 1-2 quarters. Global-wise, UBS expects tighter oil supply/ demand in H224, with an average supply deficit of 1.1Mb/d, which in our view could be a positive for rig demand. We suggest watching COSL-H (Buy) following the recent share price correction.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Financials

May Yan on Bank of Communication (3328 HK): We maintain a Neutral rating for BOCOM, but revised up our price target in Jun 2024 to reflect the better capacity for BOCOM to stabilize its NIM by managing down the high-cost legacy deposits, as well as its improved capital position after the new capital rules - even though the absolute level of NIM and capital adequacy ratio remain on the low end compared with peers. The bank's property & LGFV exposures are worth monitoring but remain manageable for the moment.

Charles Zhou on China Life Insurance (2628 HK): For H124, the insurer expressed confidence in achieving double-digit value of new business growth, implying a decent growth in Q2 despite a high base. For investment, the performance improved YoY in H124, supported by better A-share sentiment (CSI300 +0.9%; vs H123: down 0.8%). The insurer will distribute its first interim dividend this year following the new set of ‘National Nine Articles’, with <=30% payout ratio based on NPAT under new accounting standards. For full-year dividend, we believe that the payout ratio is likely >=35%.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Chen Chen on Innovent Biologics Inc: Innovent catalysts: 1) drug approvals including KRAS, ROS1, mazdutide on obesity, IGF-1R; 2) NDA submission including mazdutide on diabetes, IL-23p19; 3) potential BD.

Anita Wei on Shanghai Pharmaceutical (2607 HK) : Company is confident on outperforming peers in the upcoming Q2 results, where the competitors have generally guided down or likely to miss earnings. We expect its Q2 distribution revenue to deliver mid-single digits, and net profit to post a much higher growth given the low base last year.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Phyllis Wang on CRRC Corp (1766 HK): We like CRRC-H on back of healthy earnings outlook (supported by solid demand for rolling stock), attractive valuation (2024E/25E PER: 11x/9x) and dividend yield (5-6%). We expect rolling stock in China is still in the upcycle and it will last another 2 years, benefitting from healthy passenger traffic growth, positive trade-in-policy and healthy demand for maintenance service.

Robin Xu on Jiangsu Expressway (177 HK): On top of healthy traffic growth in 2024, we expect policy support to help a re-rating: 1) Toll Road Management Ordinance legislation may happen in H224 if not 2025, providing return protection for operating toll roads; and 2) tariff scheme for the 15th Five Year Plan period may encourage higher toll charges for road expansion.

Robin Xu on China Merchants Port Holdings (144 KH): CMPort is on our APAC Key Call list. We are the only one in the market highlighting port tariff in 2025 and every three years in the long run. Our earnings estimate premium over consensus can also be explained by more profit contribution from overseas ports as well as better than expected volume growth YTD.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Amily Guo on Dongyue Group (189 HK): We like Dongyue as: 1) We think thirdgeneration refrigerants have kicked off a price hike cycle and Dongyue is one of the major beneficiaries; 2) We expect Dongyue’s polymer business to stabilize as the price level has already fallen to historical lows; 3) Compared with A-share listed refrigerant companies, Dongyue’s valuation is more attractive.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Xinlei Li on China National Building (3323 HK): We remain cautious on CNBM given concerning cash flow outlook: 1) the company remained high leverage position with net gearing at 80% under downcycles; 2) aggressive capex plan of Rmb34bn in 2024E (vs. net loss of Rmb2bn in H124) could further weigh on its balance sheet.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Utilities

Ken Liu on ENN Energy Holding (2688 HK): We have Neutral rating on ENN Energy largely because of the lowest residential city gas exposure among peers. That said, the contribution of residential gas cost pass through to ENN’s dollar margin recovery is relatively limited versus peers.

## What our Japanese (fundamental) sector analysts think the highest/ lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Communication Services

Yijia Zhai on Konami Group: We have a Buy rating on Konami due to the company’s longer term upside from the solid expansion of eFootball, a free to play title that’s released on both mobile and console platform. We see this expansion to continue for at least another two years.

Yijia Zhai on Nexon Co Ltd: We have a Buy rating on Nexon due to the much stronger than consensus revenue and OP generated by their newly released mobile title: Dungeon and Fighter in China in mid May. We also think their newly released online RPG game the First Descendent will help company to give an upside surprise in terms of Sep quarter guidance.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Communication Services

Yijia Zhai on Cyberagent Inc: Regarding CyberAgent, the newly released mobile game Gakuen iDol M@ster (published by Bandai Namco, developed by CyberAgent) is heavily cannibalizing CyberAgent’s existing top grossing mobile title Uma Musume, which can lead to the decline in the game earnings.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Suzuki Motor Corp: We think the company’s strategy of emphasising volume growth with a focus on compact vehicles for emerging markets including India and Africa provides high visibility and appeal.

Yijia Zhai on Bandai Namco: For Bandai Namco, we think the June quarter earnings will outperform the market consensus due to the stronger than expected sales of Elden Ring DLC (released in June). But lacking other near term catalyst may lead to a pull back after the earnings.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Nissan Motor: The roll-out of new models with expected higher volume sales, is skewed toward FY3/26 and beyond, while costs are likely to emerge in FY3/25. The competitive landscape in the US is deteriorating rapidly and tools to counter prevailing market deterioration seem limited.

Kohei Takahashi on Honda Motor: The automobile operations have entered a profit downtrend excluding forex effects, reflecting stiffer competition in the US and costs related to electrification, in our view. However, the consensus forecast is for yoy OP growth in FY3/26. a peaking out of automotive profit margins is not fully priced in.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Asahi Group Holdings: Forward PBR is almost 1.0x and we expect the revision up of guidance and expect aggressive comment from management about buyback next year.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Meiji Holdings: We are Neutral on the stock because 1) of weak topline in food business, 2) they will spend a lot of research cost in 1H in pharmaceutical businesses, and 3) still unclear about the contribution of covid 19 vacinnes to earnings in 2H 2024.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Tomoko Yoshihara on Terumo Corp: We have a Buy rating on Terumo because 1) the brisk demand for key products in catheter area in major regions, 2) the positive impacts from cost reduction efforts and price hike, and 3) the solid progress for Rika project with CSL .

Kasumi Haruta on Chugai Pharmaceutical: Chugai has strong capability for drug discovery especially for antibody, and medium molecule drug discovery could be the next pillar. Growth driver hemophilia drug Hemilbra is growing steadily, offsetting rheumatism drug Actemra’s Bio Similar erosion for next few years. Chugai has a highprofile and leading oral GLP-1 agonist orforglipron and its phase 3 data readout is expected in 1H 2025.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Tomoko Yoshihara on M3 Inc: We have a Sell rating on M3 because of 1) sales for the online marketing business for pharma should remain sluggish, 2) other business including overseas business should be solid. That said, it should be difficult to offset the negative impact from the online marketing business, and 3) corporates actions such as share buyback is less likely to happen in the near term.

Fumiyoshi Sakai on Sumitomo Pharma: Struggling from lost of patent exclusivity anti psychotic drug Latuda, the company has adopted a huge restructuring program in the USA, followed by redundancy program in Japan. Massive cost reduction may help company’s short term earnings but not convincing enough to secure mid-and long term recovery.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Harunobu Goroh on Marubeni Corp: We expect good earnings progress for 1Q result.The profit growth trend to be maintained with an allocation centred on strengthening existing business areas. A rise in ROE expectations and a decline in beta will lead directly to PBR upside.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Kohei Takahashi on Hino Motors: We expect operating losses to continue for US parts until 2027. We also estimate cyclically weak sales in emerging markets due to deterioration of automobile credits quality.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Info Tech

Shingo Hirata on Kyocera Corp: Despite market expectation earlier this year, we believe it is unlikely the company to announce any major selling of KDDI shares. We also believe the fundamental will not recover strongly due to on-going inventory adjustment in passive component and substrate.

Shingo Hirata on Alps Alpine: We believe the company fundamental is challenged. In smartphone business, they are set to lose market share in their key customer business due to technology change. In automotive business, the company’s existing auto parts business is being commoditized and the company has execution risk in new business.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Materials

Harunobu Goroh on Mitsui Mining & Smelting: We expect the main products copper foil earnings would be on recovery trend in FY24 thanks to memory demand picking up and margin improvement. Zinc price and JPY depreciation will work as a positive factor as well.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Materials

Shunta Omura on Sumitomo Chemical: Highly likely to revise down FY3/25 company OP guidance due to petrochemical business as well as agrochemical and pharmaceutical business’ lower recoveries.

Harunobu Goroh on Sumitomo Metal Mining: FY24 Eanings forecast can be revised upward by high copper price and JPY depreciation. However, we concern about near term LME copper inventory building up risk, and nickel price which is another key earnings driver, is not supportive.

# What our quantamental model says about Australia

Below, we flag Australian stocks that screen well quantitatively in our quantamental framework and qualitatively based on UBS analysts' ratings.

## What our Australian (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest ranked stocks

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Shaun Cousins on Domino's Pizza Enterprises: DMP execution has been mixed over recent years following a period where COVID was an accelerant (delivery skew, peer channels closed in Japan). Recent acquisitions have created a combination of markets where DMP has been unable to execute well, with management looking somewhat stretched. Slower store gth and slower EBIT margin expansion drove lower UBS EPS estimates and make share price outperformance unlikely, yet arguably provides DMP a more sound footing for future gth. Despite these challenges, we believe share price performance (since 3-Jul-23/3-Jan-24 DMP -14.0%/-29.1% vs ASX200 +5.0%/+1.1%) and improving SSSg (ANZ strengthening, Germany performing & Japan positive albeit early) make the risk reward more balanced; Neutral.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Coles Group Ltd: Performance in the core Supermarkets division is improving (LFL sales gth to start CY24E, gross margin tailwind as theft headwind reverses, some early cost savings from Witron automated DC) yet headwinds exist across Liquor & Other divisions. The COL P/E multiple remains at a discount to WOW, albeit less than the average since Nov-18. Risk/reward balanced yet preferred vs WOW (both Neutral rated).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Woolworths Group Ltd: We are Neutral on WOW. Its 1H24E was preguided yet the shape of the core Aust Food division and concerns about sales gth with CODB pressures to continue, as well as ongoing questions on the outlook for NZ Food & Big W, weighed on investor sentiment and drive UBSe EBIT lower. Further, the Aust Food industry is facing multiple regulatory reviews (Senate enquiry, the scheduled food and grocery code of conduct review, pricing & competition review by the ACCC), while the retirement of CEO Brad Banducci, who is well regarded, was unexpected, or at least earlier than expected.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Financials

Scott Russell on Medibank Private Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) resident claims inflation remains low, and recently confirmed “low end” of 2.2-2.4% guidance for this year. This is supporting high margins and high retention rates. UBSe forecasts are above consensus, 2) Australia’s health insurance industry is enjoying persistent benefits of COVID – higher margins and higher policy growth, and 3) nonresident segment is recovering quickly as visa numbers surge and margins recover post COVID.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Financials

Scott Russell on ASX: We have a Sell Rating on ASX because 1) EBIT margins are high vs global Exchange peers, and we believe will continue to decline as competitive threat becomes clearer, 2) consensus opex and capex expectations are likely too low as the company works to upgrade CHESS system over the next 5 years, and 3) stock expensive at 25x vs global peers and vs 10Y average below 20x.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Telix Pharmaceuticals Ltd: Top pick in Australian healthcare – high growth, CF positive biotech with imminent pipeline developments.

Laura Sutcliffe on CSL Ltd: We see upside risk to 2-3yr earnings estimates as delivery on components of gross margin recovery materialises.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Nathan Reilly on ALS Ltd: We are Neutral rated on ALS with the stock trading at a 1yr fwd EV/EBITDA of 11x (LT ave. 10x) and offering FY25/26 EPS growth of 4%/10% (UBSe). We see the turnaround of Nuvisan, integration of recent acq., and a recovery in xploration activity as key catalysts for the stock into FY25. We look to the upcoming AGM in July for formal earnings guidance and further colour on key end market outlooks.

Nathan Reilly on Seven Group Holdings: We are Buy rated on SGH, with the stock offering a 3yr EBIT CAGR of 12%, and strong operating cash flows supporting further deleveraging (LTM Dec'23 PF <2.5x; FY25E 2x). The stock trades at a 1yr fwd P/E of 16x, representing a c.25% discount to the ASX200 Ind. (ex-Fins).

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Nathan Reilly on Incitec Pivot: We are Buy rated on IPL, with the stock now trading at an FY24E EV/EBITDA of c.6x, which is below its 10yr average of c.8x. Furthermore, the stock is also trading at a c.5% discount to book value vs. a 10yr premium of c.15%. We see share price support in the name as the company progresses its \$900mn on-market share buyback. The upcoming September investor day is the next potential catalyst for the stock, in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Cody Shield on Region Re Ltd: We are Buy rated on the stock. Post a significant rebasing of FY25e earnings in 2023 and early 2024, we now see the earnings outlook for RGN as robust given its non-discretionary exposure in a period of macroeconomic uncertainty.

Cody Shield on Arena Reit: We are Neutral rated on the stock on valuation grounds. ARF’s portfolio provides resilient income in a real estate subsector exposed to favourable demographic tailwinds (childcare) while also offering upside through development of new facilities.

## Valuation Method and Risk Statement

Risks of multi-asset investing include but are not limited to market risk, credit risk, interest rate risk, and foreign exchange risk. Correlations of returns among different asset classes may deviate from historical patterns. Geopolitical events and policy shocks pose risks that can reduce asset returns. Valuations may be adversely affected during times of high market volatility, thin liquidity, and economic dislocation.

KEY DEFINITIONS: Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Short-Term Ratings reflect the expected near-term (up to three months) performance of the stock and do not reflect any change in the fundamental view or investment case. Equity Price Targets have an investment horizon of 12 months.

EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES: UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core Banding Exceptions (CBE): Exceptions to the standard +/-6% bands may be granted by the Investment Review Committee (IRC). Factors considered by the IRC include the stock's volatility and the credit spread of the respective company's debt. As a result, stocks deemed to be very high or low risk may be subject to higher or lower bands as they relate to the rating. When such exceptions apply, they will be identified in the Company Disclosures table in the relevant research piece.
